```
{"author":"coderabbitai[bot]","body":"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_\n\n**Pass the secret via `env:` instead of directly interpolating `${{ secrets.* }}` in the `run:` script.**\n\nGitHub's own documentation advises to avoid passing secrets between processes from the command line, as command-line processes may be visible to other users (via `ps`) or captured by security audit events, and recommends using environment variables or other mechanisms instead.\n\nThe `${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY }}` expression is expanded server-side into the literal shell script string before the runner executes it. If the PEM value contains shell metacharacters (backticks, `$()`, etc.), they can be interpreted by bash. {"author":"coderabbitai[bot]","body":"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_\n\n**`AllPrivileges: true` violates least-privilege — scope down to what dbt actually needs.**\n\n`ALL PRIVILEGES` on a Snowflake schema grants everything except `OWNERSHIP`: `USAGE`, `MODIFY`, `MONITOR`, `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE VIEW`, `CREATE STAGE`, `CREATE SEQUENCE`, `CREATE FUNCTION`, `CREATE PROCEDURE`, `CREATE TASK`, `CREATE STREAM`, `CREATE ALERT`, and more. dbt's transformer role only realistically needs `USAGE`, `MODIFY`, `CREATE TABLE`, and `CREATE VIEW` (plus any other object types you actively use). Granting the full set is a broader attack surface than necessary.\n\nAlso note: schemas that `DBT_ROLE` creates itself are already owned by that role, so this grant is mainly relevant for pre-existing schemas (e.g., `PUBLIC`). If you rely on it for future schemas created externally, a future-grant is also needed.\n\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🔒 Proposed fix: grant only required schema-level privileges\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```diff\n-\t// Grant ALL PRIVILEGES on all schemas in CORE (for dbt to manage schemas)\n-\t_, err = snowflake.NewGrantPrivilegesToAccountRole(ctx, \"dbtGrantCoreSchemasAll\", \u0026snowflake.GrantPrivilegesToAccountRoleArgs{\n-\t\tAccountRoleName: dbtRole.FullyQualifiedName,\n-\t\tAllPrivileges:   pulumi.BoolPtr(true),\n-\t\tOnSchema: \u0026snowflake.GrantPrivilegesToAccountRoleOnSchemaArgs{\n-\t\t\tAllSchemasInDatabase: coreDb.FullyQualifiedName,\n-\t\t},\n-\t})\n+\t// Grant required privileges on all schemas in CORE\n+\t_, err = snowflake.NewGrantPrivilegesToAccountRole(ctx, \"dbtGrantCoreSchemasAll\", \u0026snowflake.GrantPrivilegesToAccountRoleArgs{\n+\t\tAccountRoleName: dbtRole.FullyQualifiedName,\n+\t\tPrivileges:      pulumi.ToStringArray([]string{\"USAGE\", \"MODIFY\", \"CREATE TABLE\", \"CREATE VIEW\"}),\n+\t\tOnSchema: \u0026snowflake.GrantPrivilegesToAccountRoleOnSchemaArgs{\n+\t\t\tAllSchemasInDatabase: coreDb.FullyQualifiedName,\n+\t\t},\n+\t})\n```\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🤖 Prompt for AI Agents\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```\nVerify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.\n\nIn `@infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go` around lines 141 - 151, The current call\nto snowflake.NewGrantPrivilegesToAccountRole uses AllPrivileges:\npulumi.BoolPtr(true) which violates least-privilege; change it to grant only the\nspecific schema privileges dbt needs (e.g., USAGE, MODIFY, CREATE TABLE, CREATE\nVIEW and any other objects you actually use) instead of ALL PRIVILEGES. Locate\nthe NewGrantPrivilegesToAccountRole invocation (dbtGrantCoreSchemasAll) and\nreplace the AllPrivileges flag with an explicit list of privileges in the\nGrantPrivilegesToAccountRoleArgs for the OnSchema target (using\ndbtRole.FullyQualifiedName and coreDb.FullyQualifiedName), and if you need\nfuture schemas consider adding a future-grant or comment about ownership\nsemantics for schemas created by DBT_ROLE.\n```\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- fingerprinting:phantom:poseidon:churro --\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- This is an auto-generated comment by CodeRabbit --\u003e","line":151,"path":"infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go"}
{"author":"coderabbitai[bot]","body":"_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟡 Minor_\n\n**Empty-string default for `private_key_path` yields a cryptic `FileNotFoundError` instead of a clear missing-variable error.**\n\nWhen `SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH` is unset (e.g., local dev targeting `snowflake`), dbt resolves the path to `\"\"` and the Snowflake connector throws `FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: ''`. Dropping the default causes dbt to fail with the much clearer `\"Env var required but not provided: 'SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH'\"`.\n\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🛡️ Proposed fix\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```diff\n-      private_key_path: \"{{ env_var('SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH', '') }}\"\n+      private_key_path: \"{{ env_var('SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH') }}\"\n```\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- suggestion_start --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e📝 Committable suggestion\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n\u003e ‼️ **IMPORTANT**\n\u003e Carefully review the code before committing. Ensure that it accurately replaces the highlighted code, contains no missing lines, and has no issues with indentation. Thoroughly test \u0026 benchmark the code to ensure it meets the requirements.\n\n```suggestion\n      private_key_path: \"{{ env_var('SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH') }}\"\n```\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- suggestion_end --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e🤖 Prompt for AI Agents\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n```\nVerify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.\n\nIn `@transform/core/profiles.yml` at line 49, The profiles.yml entry for\nprivate_key_path currently uses env_var('SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH', '') which\nsupplies an empty string default and causes a FileNotFoundError; change it to\ncall env_var('SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH') without the empty-string default so\ndbt will raise \"Env var required but not provided\" when the variable is unset\n(update the private_key_path line in the profiles.yml where private_key_path and\nenv_var are used).\n```\n\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- fingerprinting:phantom:poseidon:churro --\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- This is an auto-generated comment by CodeRabbit --\u003e","line":49,"path":"transform/core/profiles.yml"}
```

```
{"author":"gemini-code-assist[bot]","body":"## Summary of Changes\n\nHello @ta93abe, I'm Gemini Code Assist[^1]! I'm currently reviewing this pull request and will post my feedback shortly. In the meantime, here's a summary to help you and other reviewers quickly get up to speed!\n\nこのプルリクエストは、dbt-snowflakeの認証メカニズムを改善し、より堅牢で互換性のある秘密鍵認証を確立することを目的としています。`dbt-snowflake`が`private_key_content`をサポートせず、PEM形式の秘密鍵の改行が環境変数経由で破損する問題を解決するため、秘密鍵を一時ファイルに書き出し、そのパスを渡す方式に移行しました。これにより、Snowflakeへの接続が安定し、セキュリティプラクティスが向上します。\n\n### Highlights\n\n* **profiles.ymlの修正**: Snowflakeの認証設定を`private_key_content`から`private_key_path`を使用するように変更しました。\n* **dbtワークフローの更新**: CI/merge/deployの3つのdbtワークフローに、秘密鍵を一時ファイルに書き出すステップを追加しました。\n* **環境変数の利用方法変更**: 環境変数`SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY`を直接渡す方式から、ファイルパス経由で`SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`を使用する方式に切り替えました。\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eChangelog\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n* **transform/core/profiles.yml**\n    * Snowflake接続設定で`private_key_content`の代わりに`private_key_path`を使用するように変更されました。\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eIgnored Files\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n*   **Ignored by pattern:** `.github/workflows/**` (3)\n    *   .github/workflows/dbt-ci.yml\n    *   .github/workflows/dbt-deploy.yml\n    *   .github/workflows/dbt-merge.yml\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e\u003cb\u003eUsing Gemini Code Assist\u003c/b\u003e\u003c/summary\u003e\n\u003cbr\u003e\n\nThe full guide for Gemini Code Assist can be found on our [documentation page](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/review-github-code), here are some quick tips.\n\n\u003cb\u003eInvoking Gemini\u003c/b\u003e\n\nYou can request assistance from Gemini at any point by creating a comment using either `/gemini \u003ccommand\u003e` or `@gemini-code-assist \u003ccommand\u003e`. Below is a summary of the supported commands on the current page.\n\nFeature | Command | Description\n--- | --- | ---\nCode Review | `/gemini review` | Performs a code review for the current pull request in its current state.\nPull Request Summary | `/gemini summary` | Provides a summary of the current pull request in its current state.\nComment | @gemini-code-assist | Responds in comments when explicitly tagged, both in pull request comments and review comments.\nHelp | `/gemini help` | Displays a list of available commands.\n\n\u003cb\u003eCustomization\u003c/b\u003e\n\nTo customize Gemini Code Assist for GitHub experience, repository maintainers can create a configuration file and/or provide a custom code review style guide (such as PEP-8 for Python) by creating and adding files to a `.gemini/` folder in the base of the repository. Detailed instructions can be found [here](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/customize-gemini-behavior-github).\n\n\u003cb\u003eLimitations \u0026 Feedback\u003c/b\u003e\n\nGemini Code Assist may make mistakes. Please leave feedback on any instances where its feedback is incorrect or counter productive. You can react with :thumbsup: and :thumbsdown: on @gemini-code-assist comments. If you're interested in giving your feedback about your experience with Gemini Code Assist for Github and other Google products, sign up [here](https://google.qualtrics.com/jfe/form/SV_2cyuGuTWsEw84yG).\n\n\u003cb\u003eYou can also get AI-powered code generation, chat, as well as code reviews directly in the IDE at no cost with the [Gemini Code Assist IDE Extension](https://cloud.google.com/products/gemini/code-assist).\u003c/b\u003e\n\u003c/details\u003e\n\n\n[^1]: Review the [Privacy Notices](https://policies.google.com/privacy), [Generative AI Prohibited Use Policy](https://policies.google.com/terms/generative-ai/use-policy), [Terms of Service](https://policies.google.com/terms), and learn how to configure Gemini Code Assist in GitHub [here](https://developers.google.com/gemini-code-assist/docs/customize-gemini-behavior-github). {"author":"coderabbitai[bot]","body":"\u003c!-- This is an auto-generated comment: summarize by coderabbit.ai --\u003e\n\u003c!-- walkthrough_start --\u003e\n\n\u003cdetails\u003e\n\u003csummary\u003e📝 Walkthrough\u003c/summary\u003e\n\n## Walkthrough\n\nThis pull request refactors Snowflake authentication handling across CI/CD workflows and dbt configuration to use file-based private key storage instead of direct environment variables. Additionally, it introduces a new infrastructure grant providing all privileges on all CORE database schemas to the DBT_ROLE, and updates the dbt profile to support environment-driven schema configuration.\n\n## Changes\n\n|Cohort / File(s)|Summary|\n|---|---|\n|**GitHub CI/CD Workflow Updates** \u003cbr\u003e `.github/workflows/dbt-ci.yml`, `dbt-deploy.yml`, `dbt-merge.yml`|Removed SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY from top-level env sections. Added new \"Write Snowflake private key\" step that writes the secret to a temporary file with restrictive permissions (600) and exports the file path via SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH environment variable, enabling downstream steps to reference the key from a file rather than directly in env.|\n|**Snowflake Infrastructure Grant** \u003cbr\u003e `infrastructure/pulumi/snowflake.go`|Added new grant resource (dbtGrantCoreSchemasAll) granting all privileges on all schemas in the CORE database to DBT_ROLE, scoped to AllSchemasInDatabase and using AllPrivileges flag.|\n|**dbt Profile Configuration** \u003cbr\u003e `transform/core/profiles.yml`|Updated Snowflake output configuration to use `private_key_path` with environment variable reference instead of `private_key_content`, and switched schema from hardcoded `public` to environment-driven value with fallback default.|\n\n## Estimated code review effort\n\n🎯 2 (Simple) | ⏱️ ~12 minutes\n\n## Possibly related PRs\n\n- **PR `#39`**: Directly extends DBT-role/CORE-database provisioning by introducing the schema-wide grant that this PR complements.\n- **PR `#42`**: Introduced the initial REDACTED authentication mechanism that this PR refactors to use file-based credential handling.\n- **PR `#38`**: Modifies Snowflake provisioning and CI authentication, offering an alternative OIDC-based approach to the private-key-file pattern adopted in this PR.\n\n## Poem\n\n\u003e 🐰 A key hops from the air to a secure file,\\\n\u003e Permissions lock it tight with chmod style,\\\n\u003e The CORE database grants shine so new,\\\n\u003e While schemas dance with env vars too—\\\n\u003e Security whispers, infrastructure grows! > AGENT
```

PR #43 をマージし、main に戻りました。

**対応内容まとめ：**

1. **セキュリティ改善**: シークレットを step-level `env:` で渡すよう変更（シェルメタ文字のリスク回避）
2. **ファイル権限**: `umask 177` でファイル作成時に 0600 を保証（一時的な world-readable 問題を解消）
3. **最小権限**: `ALL PRIVILEGES` → `USAGE, MODIFY, CREATE TABLE, CREATE VIEW` に限定
4. **エラーメッセージ改善**: `private_key_path` の空デフォルト削除（未設定時に明確なエラー）

次のステップとして、`dim_dates` の DuckDB 固有 SQL を Snowflake 対応にする作業が残っています。

tool_result
id: toolu_01VUFET48tJPBwjaJnxyLeJu
```
     1→with date_spine as (
     2→    select
     3→        unnest(generate_series(
     4→            date '2024-01-01',
     5→            date '2024-12-31',
     6→            interval '1 day'
     7→        ))::date as date_day
     8→),
     9→
    10→final as (
    11→    select
    12→        -- Primary Key
    13→        date_day,
    14→
    15→        -- Date Attributes
    16→        extract(year from date_day) as year,
    17→        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,
    18→        extract(month from date_day) as month,
    19→        extract(week from date_day) as week_of_year,
    20→        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,
    21→        extract(dow from date_day) as day_of_week,
    22→
    23→        -- Formatted Strings
    24→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,
    25→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,
    26→        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,
    27→        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,
    28→
    29→        -- Flags
    30→        case when extract(dow from date_day) in (0, 6) then true else false end as is_weekend,
    31→        case when extract(dow from date_day) between 1 and 5 then true else false end as is_weekday,
    32→
    33→        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)
    34→        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,
    35→        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter
    36→
    37→    from date_spine
    38→)
    39→
    40→select * from final
    41→
```

> AGENT

DuckDB 固有の部分は以下の3箇所です：

**1. `generate_series()` + `unnest()` (3行目)**
```sql
unnest(generate_series(date '2024-01-01', date '2024-12-31', interval '1 day'))::date
```
Snowflake にはこれらの関数がありません。Snowflake では `GENERATOR()` + `DATEADD()` を使います。

**2. `strftime()` (24-27行目)**
```sql
strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m')
```
DuckDB の書式関数。Snowflake では `TO_CHAR()` を使います。

**3. `extract(dow from ...)` (21, 30, 31行目)**
```sql
extract(dow from date_day)
```
DuckDB の `dow` は日曜=0、土曜=6。Snowflake では `DAYOFWEEK` で、デフォルトの曜日番号体系が異なります。

> AGENT

dbt_utils 1.3.3 が入っています。`date_spine` マクロがあるので、まとめられます。

```sql
-- Before (DuckDB固有)
unnest(generate_series(date '2024-01-01', date '2024-12-31', interval '1 day'))::date

-- After (dbt_utils: DuckDB/Snowflake両対応)
{{ dbt_utils.date_spine(datepart="day", start_date="'2024-01-01'", end_date="'2025-01-01'") }}
```

`strftime` → dbt が提供するクロスDB対応はないですが、Jinja の `if` で分岐するか、シンプルに `TO_CHAR` / `strftime` を使い分ける手があります。ただ実際は **dbt_date** パッケージにもっと豊富な日付ヘルパーがあります。

一番シンプルな方法は：
- `date_spine` → **dbt_utils.date_spine** で置換
- `strftime` → `TO_CHAR` に統一（Snowflake構文、DuckDB も `TO_CHAR` をサポートしている）
- `extract(dow ...)` → `extract(dow ...)` は DuckDB/Snowflake 両方使えるが挙動が違うので `dayofweek()` 系に統一

DuckDB も `TO_CHAR` をサポートしているので、**Snowflake 構文に寄せれば両方で動きます**。やりますか？

```json
{
  "description": "Check DuckDB TO_CHAR and DOW",
  "prompt": "Search the web for:\n1. Does DuckDB support TO_CHAR for date formatting? What format strings does it use (e.g., 'YYYY-MM', 'Day', 'Month')?\n2. What does `extract(dow from date)` return in DuckDB vs Snowflake? (Sunday=0 vs Sunday=1 etc.)\n3. Does DuckDB support `dayofweek()` function?\n\nReturn the key differences and what's compatible between DuckDB and Snowflake for these functions.",

  "model": "haiku"
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01FDitchvmspzCsypbCmqmZN
```
Based on my research, here are the key differences between DuckDB and Snowflake for date formatting and day-of-week functions:

## 1. TO_CHAR / Date Formatting

**DuckDB:**
- **Does NOT support TO_CHAR** - this is a PostgreSQL/Snowflake function
- Uses `strftime(date_column, format_string)` instead
- Format strings use POSIX/C-style placeholders: `%Y`, `%m`, `%d`, `%A`, `%B`, etc.
- Example: `strftime(DATE '1992-03-02', '%d/%m/%Y')` → '02/03/1992'
- Full day/month names: `%A` (Monday), `%B` (March)

**Snowflake:**
- Uses `TO_CHAR(date_column, 'format_string')`
- Format strings use uppercase: `YYYY`, `MM`, `DD`, `Day`, `Month`
- Example: `TO_CHAR(current_date, 'YYYY-MM-DD')`

**Compatibility Notes:**
- You cannot directly convert TO_CHAR code from Snowflake to DuckDB
- Format patterns are completely different (`%Y` vs `YYYY`)

---

## 2. EXTRACT(DOW FROM date) - Day of Week Values

**DuckDB:**
- `EXTRACT(DOW FROM timestamp)` returns **0-6** (Sunday = 0, Saturday = 6)
- Alternative: `dayofweek(date)` also returns 0-6

**Snowflake:**
- `EXTRACT(DOW FROM date)` returns **1-7** (Sunday = 1, Saturday = 7)

**Critical Difference:**
- Sunday is `0` in DuckDB but `1` in Snowflake
- Saturday is `6` in DuckDB but `7` in Snowflake
- **These are NOT compatible without conversion logic**

---

## 3. ISO Day of Week

**DuckDB:**
- `EXTRACT(ISODOW FROM timestamp)` returns **1-7** (Monday = 1, Sunday = 7)
- Alternative: `isodow(date)` function
- Follows ISO 8601 standard

**Snowflake:**
- No direct ISODOW equivalent in standard functions
- You'd need to use conditional logic or custom conversion

---

## Summary Table

| Feature | DuckDB | Snowflake | Compatible? |
|---------|--------|-----------|------------|
| **Date Formatting** | `strftime()` with `%Y-%m-%d` | `TO_CHAR()` with `YYYY-MM-DD` | ❌ No |
| **Extract DOW** | `EXTRACT(DOW)` = 0-6 (Sun=0) | `EXTRACT(DOW)` = 1-7 (Sun=1) | ❌ No |
| **dayofweek()** | Returns 0-6 | Not available | ❌ No |
| **ISO Weekday** | `EXTRACT(ISODOW)` = 1-7 (Mon=1) | Not standard | ⚠️ Partial |

---

## Migration Implications

If you're migrating from Snowflake to DuckDB or vice versa:

1. **Date formatting**: Replace `TO_CHAR(date, 'YYYY-MM-DD')` with `strftime(date, '%Y-%m-%d')`
2. **Day of week logic**: Add +1 or conditional logic to handle the 0-6 vs 1-7 difference
3. **Testing**: Verify edge cases around day numbering (especially when used in GROUP BY or JOIN logic)

---

**Sources:**
- [DuckDB Date Format Functions](https://duckdb.org/docs/stable/sql/functions/dateformat)
- [DuckDB Date Part Functions](https://duckdb.org/docs/stable/sql/functions/datepart)
- [DuckDB Date Functions](https://duckdb.org/docs/stable/sql/functions/date)
- [How to Format Dates in DuckDB](https://database.guide/how-to-format-dates-in-duckdb/)
- [Snowflake Date and Time Input/Output Formats](https://docs.snowflake.com/en/sql-reference/date-time-input-output)
```

```
     1→with date_spine as (
     2→    select
     3→        unnest(generate_series(
     4→            date '2024-01-01',
     5→            date '2024-12-31',
     6→            interval '1 day'
     7→        ))::date as date_day
     8→),
     9→
    10→final as (
    11→    select
    12→        -- Primary Key
    13→        date_day,
    14→
    15→        -- Date Attributes
    16→        extract(year from date_day) as year,
    17→        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,
    18→        extract(month from date_day) as month,
    19→        extract(week from date_day) as week_of_year,
    20→        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,
    21→        extract(dow from date_day) as day_of_week,
    22→
    23→        -- Formatted Strings
    24→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,
    25→        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,
    26→        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,
    27→        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,
    28→
    29→        -- Flags
    30→        case when extract(dow from date_day) in (0, 6) then true else false end as is_weekend,
    31→        case when extract(dow from date_day) between 1 and 5 then true else false end as is_weekday,
    32→
    33→        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)
    34→        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,
    35→        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter
    36→
    37→    from date_spine
    38→)
    39→
    40→select * from final
    41→
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**dbt_utils.date_spine**: `generate_series` + `unnest` の代わりに使えるクロスDB対応マクロ。内部的に各アダプタ向けの SQL を生成してくれます。出力カラムは `date_day`。

**書式関数の違い**: `strftime`（DuckDB）と `TO_CHAR`（Snowflake）はフォーマット文字列が全く異なるため、Jinja の `target.type` で分岐するのが定番パターンです。`extract(dow ...)` は両方とも 0=日曜〜6=土曜で共通なので、そのまま使えます。
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01YTvCDchPMi9kjmrCmzNPpY
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql",
  "content": "with date_spine as (\n    {{ dbt_utils.date_spine(\n        datepart=\"day\",\n        start_date=\"cast('2024-01-01' as date)\",\n        end_date=\"cast('2025-01-01' as date)\"\n    ) }}\n),\n\nfinal as (\n    select\n        -- Primary Key\n        date_day,\n\n        -- Date Attributes\n        extract(year from date_day) as year,\n        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,\n        extract(month from date_day) as month,\n        extract(week from date_day) as week_of_year,\n        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,\n        extract(dow from date_day) as day_of_week,\n\n        -- Formatted Strings\n        {% if target.type == 'snowflake' %}\n        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY-MM') as year_month,\n        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY') || '-Q' || to_char(date_day, 'Q') as year_quarter,\n        to_char(date_day, 'MMMM') as month_name,\n        to_char(date_day, 'DY') as day_name,\n        {% else %}\n        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,\n        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,\n        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,\n        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,\n        {% endif %}\n\n        -- Flags\n        case when extract(dow from date_day) in (0, 6) then true else false end as is_weekend,\n        case when extract(dow from date_day) between 1 and 5 then true else false end as is_weekday,\n\n        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)\n        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,\n        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter\n\n    from date_spine\n)\n\nselect * from final\n"
}
```

```
{"author":"gemini-code-assist[bot]","body":"![high](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/high-priority.svg)\n\nSnowflakeとDuckDBの間で `day_name` カラムに不整合があります。`strftime(date_day, '%A')` を使用するDuckDBの実装では完全な曜日名（例: 'Monday'）が返されますが、`to_char(date_day, 'DY')` を使用するSnowflakeの実装では省略された大文字の曜日名（例: 'MON'）が返されます。データベース間で一貫した出力を確保するために、Snowflakeで完全な曜日名を生成するフォーマットを使用すべきです。これにより、両方のデータベースで同じ形式の曜日名が保証されます。\n\n```sql\n        to_char(date_day, 'Day') as day_name,\n```","line":27,"path":"transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql"}
{"author":"gemini-code-assist[bot]","body":"![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)\n\nハードコードされた日付が使用されています。このモデルをより柔軟で再利用可能にするために、dbt変数を使用することを検討してください。これにより、例えば`dbt_project.yml`で日付範囲を設定するなど、モデルのコードを変更することなく簡単に日付範囲を変更できるようになります。\n\n```sql\n    {{ dbt_utils.date_spine(\n        datepart=\"day\",\n        start_date=\"cast('\" ~ var('dim_dates_start_date', '2024-01-01') ~ \"' as date)\",\n        end_date=\"cast('\" ~ var('dim_dates_end_date', '2025-01-01') ~ \"' as date)\"\n    ) }}\n```","line":6,"path":"transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql"}
{"author":"gemini-code-assist[bot]","body":"![medium](https://www.gstatic.com/codereviewagent/medium-priority.svg)\n\n現在のJinjaロジックは、Snowflake以外のデータベースターゲットが`strftime`をサポートしていると想定しています。これは安全な想定ではありません。なぜなら、BigQueryやRedshiftのような多くのデータベースは`strftime`をサポートしておらず、代わりに`FORMAT_DATE`や`TO_CHAR`を使用するからです。モデルをより堅牢にし、新しいデータベースターゲットが追加された際の将来のエラーを防ぐために、各データベースタイプでどのロジックを使用するかを明示的にする方が良いです。\n\n```sql\n        {% if target.type == 'snowflake' %}\n        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY-MM') as year_month,\n        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY') || '-Q' || to_char(date_day, 'Q') as year_quarter,\n        to_char(date_day, 'MMMM') as month_name,\n        to_char(date_day, 'DY') as day_name,\n        {% elif target.type == 'duckdb' %}\n        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,\n        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,\n        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,\n        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,\n        {% else %}\n        {{ exceptions.raise_compiler_error(\"Unsupported target database for date formatting in dim_dates: \" ~ target.type) }}\n        {% endif %}\n```","line":33,"path":"transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql"}
{"author":"Copilot","body":"The Snowflake format string 'DY' produces abbreviated day names (e.g., \"Mon\", \"Tue\"), while DuckDB's '%A' produces full day names (e.g., \"Monday\", \"Tuesday\"). This creates inconsistent output between databases for the same column. Change 'DY' to 'DAY' in Snowflake to match the full day name format.\n```suggestion\n        to_char(date_day, 'DAY') as day_name,\n```","line":27,"path":"transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql"}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ta93abe/Developer/github.com/ta93abe/data-engineering-with-cloudflare/transform/core/models/marts/core/dim_dates.sql",
  "content": "with date_spine as (\n    {{ dbt_utils.date_spine(\n        datepart=\"day\",\n        start_date=\"cast('\" ~ var('dim_dates_start_date', '2024-01-01') ~ \"' as date)\",\n        end_date=\"cast('\" ~ var('dim_dates_end_date', '2025-01-01') ~ \"' as date)\"\n    ) }}\n),\n\nfinal as (\n    select\n        -- Primary Key\n        date_day,\n\n        -- Date Attributes\n        extract(year from date_day) as year,\n        extract(quarter from date_day) as quarter,\n        extract(month from date_day) as month,\n        extract(week from date_day) as week_of_year,\n        extract(day from date_day) as day_of_month,\n        extract(dow from date_day) as day_of_week,\n\n        -- Formatted Strings\n        {% if target.type == 'snowflake' %}\n        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY-MM') as year_month,\n        to_char(date_day, 'YYYY') || '-Q' || to_char(date_day, 'Q') as year_quarter,\n        to_char(date_day, 'MMMM') as month_name,\n        to_char(date_day, 'DAY') as day_name,\n        {% elif target.type == 'duckdb' %}\n        strftime(date_day, '%Y-%m') as year_month,\n        strftime(date_day, '%Y-Q') || extract(quarter from date_day) as year_quarter,\n        strftime(date_day, '%B') as month_name,\n        strftime(date_day, '%A') as day_name,\n        {% else %}\n        {{ exceptions.raise_compiler_error(\"Unsupported target type for dim_dates: \" ~ target.type) }}\n        {% endif %}\n\n        -- Flags\n        case when extract(dow from date_day) in (0, 6) then true else false end as is_weekend,\n        case when extract(dow from date_day) between 1 and 5 then true else false end as is_weekday,\n\n        -- Fiscal (assuming fiscal year = calendar year)\n        extract(year from date_day) as fiscal_year,\n        extract(quarter from date_day) as fiscal_quarter\n\n    from date_spine\n)\n\nselect * from final\n"
}
```

> AGENT

PR #44 マージ完了。main に戻りました。

**対応内容：**
- `dim_dates`: `dbt_utils.date_spine` + Jinja 条件分岐でクロスDB対応
- `DY` → `DAY` で Snowflake でも完全曜日名
- 日付範囲を `var()` で設定可能に
- 未対応 DB は明示的なコンパイルエラー
- `_seeds__schema.yml`: dbt 1.11 の deprecation 警告を解消
